- `CHECK-noisemeas-enbw`: inputs (filter type, order m, f3dB Hz) -> B = f3dB*(pi/2m)/sin(pi/2m) for Butterworth (1.22*f3dB for two cascaded RCs) -> report B; used as the B in all noise formulas -> source rows (AOE-2671, AOE-2672).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-integrated-noise`: inputs (enH V/rtHz, fc Hz, f1 Hz, f2 Hz, vn_spec V rms) -> vn = enH*sqrt(f2 - f1 + fc*ln(f2/f1)) -> pass if vn <= vn_spec -> margin = vn_spec/vn - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2681, AOE-2679).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-1f-corner`: inputs (enL V/rtHz at fL Hz, enH V/rtHz) -> fc = fL*((enL/enH)^2 - 1) -> report; flag if fc > lowest signal frequency -> source rows (AOE-2683).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-pp-to-rms`: inputs (Vn_pp V, 0.1-10 Hz) -> Vn_rms = Vn_pp/6 -> compare with budget -> source rows (AOE-2680).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-snr`: inputs (Vs V rms, Rs ohm, en, in, B Hz, T K, SNR_req dB) -> SNR = 10*log10(Vs^2/((en^2 + (in*Rs)^2 + 4kTRs)*B)) -> pass if SNR >= SNR_req -> margin = SNR - SNR_req (dB) -> source rows (AOE-2659).
- `CHECK-ldo-output-cap-esr`: inputs (regulator ESR_min, ESR_max ohm; output capacitor ESR ohm; series R ohm) -> ESR_eff = ESR_cap + R_series -> pass if ESR_min <= ESR_eff <= ESR_max -> margin = min(ESR_eff/ESR_min, ESR_max/ESR_eff) - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2668, AOE-2673).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-prbs-period`: inputs (n bits, f_clk Hz, T_min s) -> T = (2^n - 1)/f_clk -> pass if T >= T_min -> margin = T/T_min - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2666).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-rs-for-in`: inputs (in_expected A/rtHz, en V/rtHz, Rs ohm, T K) -> require in*Rs > en and in > sqrt(4kT/Rs) -> pass if both ratios >= 3 (the factor 3 is a low-confidence reading of "preferably much larger") -> margin = min(in*Rs/en, in/sqrt(4kT/Rs))/3 - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2685, AOE-2687).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-rs-bandwidth`: inputs (Rs ohm, C_in F, f_meas Hz, Ib A, G, Vsat V) -> f_BW = 1/(2*pi*Rs*C_in); V_out_dc = Ib*Rs*G -> pass if f_BW >= f_meas and V_out_dc < Vsat -> margin = min(f_BW/f_meas, Vsat/V_out_dc) - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2690).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-lockin-bandwidth`: inputs (tau s, sweep feature width in time t_feat s) -> B = 1/tau (window) -> pass if t_feat >= several tau (sweep no faster than the filter can respond) -> margin = t_feat/tau - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2699).
- `CHECK-noisemeas-adc-quantization`: inputs (LSB V, analog noise vn V rms) -> sigma_q = LSB/sqrt(12); total = sqrt(vn^2 + sigma_q^2) -> report; pass if sigma_q <= vn (quantization not dominant; criterion low-confidence) -> source rows (AOE-2696).
- `CHECK-ground-supply-noise`: inputs (e_supply(f) V/rtHz, PSRR(f) dB, en_in V/rtHz, Gv) -> e_out_supply = e_supply*10^(-PSRR/20)*Gv_supply (Gv_supply ~1 for 'unamplified' coupling) ; e_out_signal = en_in*Gv -> pass if e_out_supply <= e_out_signal/3 (factor 3 low-confidence) -> margin = e_out_signal/(3*e_out_supply) - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2701).
- `CHECK-ground-capmult-headroom`: inputs (V_ripple_pp V at full load, V_drop_Q1 V) -> pass if V_drop_Q1 > V_ripple_pp and V_drop_Q1 <= 2 V (0.6-2 V design range) -> margin = V_drop_Q1/V_ripple_pp - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2703, AOE-2704).
- `CHECK-ground-crosscoupling-cap`: inputs (Zout ohm at the aggressor's frequency, dV/dt V/s of aggressor, V_err V allowed, Cc F extracted) -> Cc_max = V_err/(Zout*dV/dt) -> pass if Cc <= Cc_max -> margin = Cc_max/Cc - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2723).
- `CHECK-ground-pseudodiff`: inputs (Gv, V_ground_noise V, allowed input-referred error V) -> error_in = V_ground_noise/Gv -> pass if error_in <= allowed -> margin = allowed/error_in - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2713, AOE-2715).
- `CHECK-ground-guard-needed`: inputs (Rs ohm, C_in F, f_sig Hz) -> f_roll = 1/(2*pi*Rs*C_in) -> pass (no guard needed) if f_roll >= 10*f_sig (factor 10 low-confidence); else require guarded input -> margin = f_roll/(10*f_sig) - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2722).
- `CHECK-ground-isolation-rating`: inputs (V_iso rating V, expected ground difference V, required BW Hz, iso-amp BW Hz) -> pass if V_iso >= V_diff and BW_iso >= BW_req -> margin = min(V_iso/V_diff, BW_iso/BW_req) - 1 -> source rows (AOE-2721).

### Ch.9 §9.1-§9.5: linear regulators, heat and SOA, ac line to unregulated dc (pp. 594-636; lines 35530-38215)
- `CHECK-linreg-zener-shunt-bias`: inputs (V+min, V+max (V), Vz (V), R (ohm), I_load,max (A), I_z,min (A), P_z,rating (W), P_R,rating (W)) -> I_z(worst) = (V+min - Vz)/R - I_load,max; P_z = Vz*(V+max - Vz)/R; P_R = (V+max - Vz)^2/R -> pass if I_z(worst) >= I_z,min and P_z <= P_z,rating and P_R <= P_R,rating -> margin = min(I_z(worst)/I_z,min, P_z,rating/P_z, P_R,rating/P_R) - 1 -> source rows AOE-2725, AOE-2726.
- `CHECK-linreg-headroom`: inputs (V_in,min at low line including ripple trough (V), V_out (V), V_dropout,max (V) from datasheet) -> V_in,min - V_out -> pass if >= V_dropout,max (723: 3 V; LM317: 2 V; LM1117: 1.2 V) -> margin = (V_in,min - V_out - V_dropout)/V_dropout -> source rows AOE-2739, AOE-2744, AOE-2754.
- `CHECK-linreg-pass-dissipation`: inputs (V_in,max (V), V_out (V), I_out,max (A), P_allow(Ta) (W)) -> P = (V_in,max - V_out)*I_out,max -> pass if P <= P_allow -> margin = P_allow/P - 1 -> source rows AOE-2740, AOE-2741.
- `CHECK-linreg-723-derating`: inputs (Ta (C), V_in, V_out (V), I_out (A)) -> P_max = 1 W - 0.0083*(Ta - 25); P = (V_in - V_out)*I_out (+ quiescent) -> pass if P <= P_max and I_out <= 0.15 A -> margin = P_max/P - 1 -> source rows AOE-2738, AOE-2740.
- `CHECK-linreg-current-limit-resistor`: inputs (I_rated (A), Rcl (ohm), Vbe_on (V, default 0.6)) -> I_limit = Vbe_on/Rcl -> pass if I_rated < I_limit <= k*I_rated (k chosen by designer; "somewhat larger") and P_Rcl = I_limit^2*Rcl <= rating -> margin = I_limit/I_rated - 1 -> source rows AOE-2734.
- `CHECK-linreg-lm317-divider`: inputs (R1, R2 (ohm), I_adj,max (A, default 100e-6), Vref (V, 1.25 +/-2%), resistor tol) -> Vout = Vref*(1 + R2/R1) + I_adj*R2; I_div = Vref/R1 -> pass if I_div >= 10 mA (or external min load present) and worst-case Vout within spec -> margin = spec half-width - worst-case deviation -> source rows AOE-2751, AOE-2752, AOE-2753.
- `CHECK-linreg-bypass-present`: inputs (per regulator: C_in, C_out (F), datasheet minima, polarity) -> compare -> pass if C_in >= min (or source bypass within close distance) and C_out >= min (LM317 >= 1 uF recommended; LM1117 >= 10 uF) -> margin = C/C_min - 1 -> source rows AOE-2742, AOE-2746, AOE-2754, AOE-2755.
- `CHECK-linreg-dual-rail-reverse-diodes`: inputs (netlist: regulator outputs of both polarities sharing a load) -> pass if each output has a reverse-biased Schottky to ground -> source rows AOE-2747.
- `CHECK-linreg-zener-bias-filter`: inputs (R1a, R1b (ohm), C1 (F), f_ripple (Hz)) -> tau = par(R1a,R1b)*C1 -> pass if tau >= 10/f_ripple (">> ripple period"; factor 10 is a low-confidence quantification) -> source rows AOE-2736.
- `CHECK-linreg-adj-error`: inputs (Vref (V), Vref tol (%), R1, R2 (ohm), resistor tol (%), I_adj,max (A)) -> worst-case V_out = Vref*(1 + R2/R1) + I_adj*R2 over tolerance corners; fractional ADJ error = I_adj*par(R1,R2)/Vref -> pass if worst-case V_out within the rail spec -> margin = (spec half-width - worst deviation)/spec half-width -> source rows AOE-2763, AOE-2768.
- `CHECK-linreg-soft-start`: inputs (I_charge (A) = Vref/R1 or Vbe/R3, C_adj (F), required ramp (V/s)) -> S = I_charge/C_adj -> pass if S <= required ramp and protection diode present when C_adj used -> margin = required/S - 1 -> source rows AOE-2770, AOE-2769.
- `CHECK-ldo-output-cap-esr`: inputs (C_out (F), ESR_min/max of chosen cap over temperature (ohm), datasheet C_min, ESR window, cap dielectric) -> pass if C_out >= C_min and ESR window overlaps the datasheet window at all load currents; fail if a ceramic > 1000 pF sits directly on an ESR-compensated LDO output without 0.1-2 ohm series R -> margin = min(C_out/C_min - 1, ESR_min,cap/ESR_min,ds - 1, ESR_max,ds/ESR_max,cap - 1) -> source rows AOE-2785, AOE-2804, AOE-2805.
- `CHECK-ldo-vin-abs-max`: inputs (V_in steady max, V_in transient peak (V), regulator V_in(max) rating, environment = automotive?) -> pass if V_in peak <= rating (CMOS often 5.5 V; load dump requires >= 50 V-class, load-dump-rated part) -> margin = rating/V_in,peak - 1 -> source rows AOE-2799, AOE-2802, AOE-2803.
- `CHECK-ldo-upstream-current-limit`: inputs (regulator I_limit,max (A), upstream fuse/trace/source ratings (A)) -> pass if every upstream element tolerates I_limit,max (e.g. 14 A for TPS755xx) -> margin = rating/I_limit,max - 1 -> source rows AOE-2784.
- `CHECK-ldo-dropout-ground-current`: inputs (I_gnd normal, I_gnd at dropout (A) from datasheet, battery capacity (Ah), time spent in/near dropout (h)) -> charge lost = I_gnd,dropout*t -> pass if battery-life requirement met including dropout period -> source rows AOE-2801.
- `CHECK-thermal-heatsink-rsa`: inputs (P (W), Ta,max (C), Tj,target (C), R_thJC, R_thCS (C/W), heatsink R_thSA,datasheet (C/W), orientation) -> R_thSA,max = (Tj,target - Ta,max)/P - R_thJC - R_thCS; R_thSA,eff = R_thSA,ds*k_orient (1.0 vertical, 1.2 horizontal, 1.45 fins up, 1.7 fins down) -> pass if R_thSA,eff <= R_thSA,max -> margin = R_thSA,max/R_thSA,eff - 1 -> source rows AOE-2758, AOE-2824, AOE-2827, AOE-2832.
- `CHECK-thermal-junction-temp`: inputs (P, Ta,max, R_thJC, R_thCS, R_thSA, Tj,max (C), derating target e.g. Tj,max - 25 C) -> Tj = Ta + P*(sum R_th) -> pass if Tj <= target -> margin = (Tj,max - Tj) (C) -> source rows AOE-2758, AOE-2823.
- `CHECK-thermal-smt-foil`: inputs (package, P (W), copper area (cm2)) -> pass if P <= 3 W for SOT-223/DPAK/D2PAK on >= 6 cm2, or P <= 0.5 W for smaller SMT packages on similar area (rough guide, conf medium) -> margin = P_allow/P - 1 -> source rows AOE-2829.
- `CHECK-derating-pulse-soa`: inputs (pulse power P_pk (W), pulse width t_p (s), Z_th(t_p) from datasheet (C/W), Tc (C), Tj,max) -> Tj,pk = Tc + P_pk*Z_th(t_p) -> pass if Tj,pk <= Tj,max and (V, I) inside the SOA curve for t_p (BJT: include second breakdown) -> margin = Tj,max - Tj,pk -> source rows AOE-2834, AOE-2835.
- `CHECK-currsrc-lm317`: inputs (R (ohm), V_drop,min, V_drop,max (V), P_allow (W)) -> I = 1.25/R + 50e-6; pass if 5e-3 <= I <= 1.5, V_drop,min >= 3 V, V_drop,max <= 40 V and V_drop,max*I <= P_allow -> source rows AOE-2815.
- `CHECK-mains-fuse`: inputs (I_rms,line measured true-rms at max load (A), fuse rating (A), fuse type) -> pass if rating >= 1.5*I_rms and type = slow-blow -> margin = rating/(1.5*I_rms) - 1 -> source rows AOE-2840, AOE-2841.
- `CHECK-mains-xcap-bleeder`: inputs (C_X (F), R_bleed (ohm), V_line (Vrms), standby budget (W)) -> tau = R_bleed*C_X; P = V_line^2/R_bleed -> pass if tau < 1 s (and P within standby budget, or active discharge IC used) -> margin = 1 s/tau - 1 -> source rows AOE-2846.
- `CHECK-rectifier-reservoir`: inputs (I_dc (A), f_line (Hz), V_ripple,pp allowed (V), C_nom (F), tol_minus (default -30%), V_pk at high line no load (V), cap V rating, cap ripple rating (Arms), ESR (ohm)) -> C_req = I_dc/(2*f_line*V_ripple); C_min = C_nom*(1 + tol_minus); I_rms,cap ~ 2*I_dc -> pass if C_min >= C_req, V_rating >= V_pk(high line, no load), ripple rating >= I_rms,cap; report P_ESR = I_rms^2*ESR -> margin = min(C_min/C_req, V_rating/V_pk, I_rating/I_rms) - 1 -> source rows AOE-2791, AOE-2853, AOE-2857, AOE-2859.
- `CHECK-rectifier-transformer-rms`: inputs (I_dc (A), transformer rms rating (A)) -> pass if rating >= 2*I_dc (capacitor-input bridge; measured 3.95 Arms for 2 A dc) -> margin = rating/(2*I_dc) - 1 -> source rows AOE-2790, AOE-2854, AOE-2866.
- `CHECK-rectifier-low-line-trough`: inputs (V_rms,sec,nom (V), line tolerance (-10% normal, -20% brownout), V_D (V), V_ripple,pp (V), loaded drop allowance (V), V_out, V_do (V)) -> V_trough = sqrt(2)*V_rms,sec*(1 + tol) - 2*V_D - drop - V_ripple,pp -> pass if V_trough >= V_out + V_do -> margin = V_trough - (V_out + V_do) (V) -> source rows AOE-2739, AOE-2744, AOE-2851, AOE-2852.

## 4. Verification procedures & plots
### Ch.5 §5.10-§5.14: choosing precision op-amps, auto-zero amplifiers, DMM front ends, difference amplifiers (pp. 319-356; lines 18822-21122)
- Offset measurement (Fig. 5.36, p.323): DUT in x1000 gain with 10 ohm input resistances; Vos = Vout/1000; 200 ohm series output resistor if cabled. Repeat over temperature to extract TCVos (not production tested, AOE-2016).
- Bias/offset current (Fig. 5.39, p.326): alternate shorting Rs1/Rs2; Ib = dVout / (gain * Rs) with Ib*Rs >= tens of mV.
- fA-level Ib (Fig. 5.40, p.326): plot Vout vs time (x = s, y = V); slope * C = Ib. Good: straight ramp; air-dielectric C; relay reset; metal enclosure.
- Noise-density plot (Fig. 5.37): x = frequency 0.1 Hz-10 kHz log, y = en (nV/sqrt(Hz)) log; read white level and 1/f corner. Integrated-noise plot (Fig. 5.54): x = upper band limit, y = Vn rms, lower limit 0.01 Hz; rank candidates at the circuit's actual bandwidth.
- Auto-zero output spikes (Figs. 5.49-5.50, p.334-336): configure G = 100 with input to ground through Rs = 100 ohm and again through 1 Mohm; scope the output (2 mV/div, 100 us/div for 100 ohm; 100 mV/div for 1 Mohm). Good: no spikes above the budget over the full circuit bandwidth. Classic AZ parts show clock feedthrough; expect nA-scale input current spikes.
- Auto-zero noise spectra (Fig. 5.52, p.336-337): measure en with Rs = 0 and in with a large Rs; x = frequency (log), y = nV/sqrt(Hz) and fA/sqrt(Hz). Look for the clock line and harmonics (e.g. 9 kHz on MCP6V06) and compare in with the datasheet (10 Hz) value.
- Integrated noise vs bandwidth (Fig. 5.54, p.338): x = lowpass bandwidth 0.001 Hz-100 kHz, y = rms noise; AZ curves fall as sqrt(bandwidth), conventional parts plateau below the 1/f corner (lower limit 0.01 Hz). Pick the part that is lowest at the design bandwidth.
- Very-low-frequency time traces (Fig. 5.53): chopper vs conventional at 0.33 Hz BW (10 s/div) and 33 Hz BW (0.1 s/div): chopper wins at the narrow bandwidth, loses at 100x bandwidth.
- Overload recovery: drive the output into saturation, release, measure time to return within the error band; compare with Table 5.6 tr (4 us to 20 ms).
- Distortion vs frequency (Figs. 5.43-5.46): x = frequency 10 Hz-1 MHz (log), y = THD or THD+N (% or ppm, log). Record level, load, Vcm, gain and analyzer filter; use the loop-gain-reduction fixture (divide by 100) for sub-100 ppm; check source-resistance dependence (Rs = 0 vs 600 ohm) for JFET non-inverting stages.
- CMRR vs frequency (Fig. 5.73): drive both inputs with the same sine through the actual source impedances, sweep 10 Hz-1 MHz; y = CMRR (dB) = 20*log10(Gdiff*Vcm/Vout). Good: meets requirement at the highest interference frequency, with realistic source imbalance.
- PSRR per rail: inject ripple on one rail at a time (100-120 Hz and harmonics, plus switching frequencies), measure input-referred output change; expect one rail (the compensation-cap rail) to be worse (OPA277: extra 25 dB on negative rail).
- Gohm-source bias current vs input capacitance (fn 52): sweep a shunt Cs at the input (2-500 pF) with Rs = 1 Gohm and record the offset; find the zero crossing (22 pF for LMP2021).
- 34420A-style nV measurement: 100 PLC integration (1.67 s, ~0.6 Hz), then average N readings; expect rms to fall as sqrt(N) until 1/f or drift dominates.

### Ch.5 §5.15-§5.17 + Review: instrumentation and fully differential amplifiers (pp. 356-390; lines 21123-23445)
- INA CMRR vs frequency (Fig. 5.82/5.84 style): drive both inputs with the same sine (CM) from a low-Z source; sweep log f 10 Hz-1 MHz (and at least the actual CM interference frequency, e.g. 50/60 Hz and the PWM frequency); repeat at each gain setting and with source-impedance imbalance inserted in one leg (0, 1 k, 10 k); y = 20*log10(Gdiff*Vcm/Vout,RTO) in dB. Good: CMRR at f_cm >= CHECK-inamp-cmrr-required, curve not collapsing below the band of interest. Filter-node parts should show flattening. (p.364-365)
- INA headroom map: sweep Vcm over the full range at each gain with full-scale Vdiff; plot max undistorted output vs Vcm (datasheet "max output voltage vs common-mode input" graph). Good: operating box fully inside; watch the AD623-type collapse (1.0 V at Vcm = 0, G = 10). (p.366)
- Protection abuse test ("beat it up, brutally"): continuous +/-250 V dc (or 240 Vac) on each input; 350-500 V steps with ~1 ns rise; monitor op-amp input current (< Iin,max, e.g. +/-5 mA), MOSFET Vgs (< +/-20 V), limiter dissipation, recovery time, output phase reversal. Good: no damage or parameter shift after repeated insults. (p.361-363)
- RF susceptibility: with the high-gain front end powered and cabled as in use, energize nearby switching supplies/radios, grip cables, touch nodes with a pencil; log dc offset shift. Good: shift below the offset budget; otherwise add input RC/ferrite filtering. (p.365-366)
- In-system calibration verification: apply cal relays (zero, reference); verify residual gain error < 0.1% and offset <= 10 uV after correction across temperature. (p.360)
- Current-source (Fig. 5.87) loop test: output current vs load voltage (compliance) and frequency response/step response with the anticipated load impedances; check for ringing/oscillation; verify error <= Vos/R_sense. (p.367)
- FDA small- and large-signal response: gain vs frequency at the design gain and Rf/Rg with ~100 mV and ~2 Vpp outputs; report peaking (dB), -0.1 dB and -3 dB bandwidths. Good: peaking within spec, flatness BW >= requirement, large-signal BW >= f_signal. (p.378-386)
- FDA/ADC driver distortion: HD2 and HD3 separately vs frequency (to 100 MHz for fast chains) at the design's gain, load, amplitude and supply (datasheet conditions are not standardized). Good: THD below the ADC requirement (e.g. <= -80 dB for 16-bit). (p.386)
- ADC driver noise: ADC FFT/histogram with the driver input terminated; integrated noise vs LSB; compare to CHECK-adc-driver-noise-vs-lsb. (p.382, 386-387)
- ADC input settling/kickback: transient sim or measurement of the driver + series R + shunt C + ADC sampling-cap model over one conversion; verify settling to < 1/2 LSB before sample. (p.374, 380-381)
- FDA input CM sweep: transient sim of the single-ended-to-differential stage over the input extremes; plot V(+)/V(-) pin voltages vs time against the input CM limits. (p.378)
- Terminated input: measure return loss / Zin at the input connector over the band; verify gain referred to the terminated amplitude. (p.382-383)

### Ch.6 Filters + Review (pp. 391-424; lines 23446-25110)
- Filter magnitude response: y = gain (dB), x = log frequency, normalized to fc (Figs. 6.2C, 6.20, 6.30); for Chebyshev/elliptic also plot linear-scale gain over the passband to see ripple (+0/-0.1 dB = +/-0.6% amplitude, Fig. 6.11). Pass: passband ripple within spec up to fc, attenuation >= A_min at f_stop (Fig. 6.23 spec box). Source p.392, 396, 403.
- Anti-alias verification: overlay filter response with markers at fs/2 and at fs - f_pass_edge; read attenuation (book: 20 dB at 1.25 MHz, +16 dB more at 1.5 MHz for fs = 2.5 Msps); calculated and measured should agree. Source p.395-396, Figs. 6.10-6.11.
- Phase: y = phase lag (deg), x = LINEAR frequency (phase plots best on linear axis); group delay tau_g = -dphi/domega vs linear frequency, normalized as f/fc and tau/T (Fig. 6.25). Good = flat delay across passband (Bessel). Source p.400, 404.
- Step response: 1 V step at t = 0 (Fig. 6.26/6.27); measure 0-90% rise time, overshoot %, settling to 1% and 0.1%; compare with Table 6.1 values scaled by 1/fc. Source p.405-406.
- Component-tolerance corners / Monte Carlo on R and C (Fig. 6.22): plot envelope of responses; pass if worst case meets the passband and stopband spec. Source p.402.
- Capacitor ESR (and ESL) in SPICE for RC supply filters: compare ideal vs datasheet-ESR models from 10 Hz to many MHz (Fig. 6.4); expect an attenuation floor where ESR ~ 1/(omega*C). Source p.392-393.
- Op-amp non-ideality in active filters: simulate with a real op-amp model (e.g. LF411, fT = 4 MHz, fc = 4 kHz) to far beyond fc; VCVS shows a stopband floor from rising op-amp output impedance, MFB does not (Figs. 6.36-6.37); try larger R, output buffer from the non-inverting node, or an extra passive RC. Source p.413-414.
- Simulated inductor (GIC/gyrator): plot abs(Z) and phase of the simulated inductor vs frequency with the real op-amp; inductive region should extend well beyond the filter band (valid only to a few % of fT); look for the capacitive region and self-resonance (Fig. 6.15). Source p.398.
- Notch filters: measure notch depth and frequency vs temperature; trim one component (50k trimmer in Fig. 6.40B) for maximum depth; check response 2x and 4x away from the notch (twin-T: -10 dB, -3 dB). Source p.414-415.
- Switched-capacitor filters: output spectrum with signal absent (clock feedthrough, expect 10-25 mV at f_clk); inject tones near f_clk +/- passband to confirm the input anti-alias prefilter; output noise spectrum (LTC1062 plot Fig. 6.47); distortion vs output amplitude near the rails. Source p.417-418.
- Series-LC sine converter / PLL trap: scope input vs output (Fig. 6.7: 400 ns/div) and spectrum-analyzer check of reference spurs at f_out +/- f_comparison (78.0 +/- 0.2 MHz). Source p.394-395.
- FIR/IIR: plot calculated response on linear and log-log axes (Figs. 6.49-6.50), compare windows at fixed N (Fig. 6.51); confirm stopband with the actual fixed-point arithmetic word length and the ADC's linearity. Source p.421.
- Design tools named by the book for generating/validating designs: TI FilterPro, LTC FilterCAD (and LTC AN38, DN245), ADI Analog Filter Wizard, Uwe Beis filter pages (beis.de), York mkfilter/lcfilter, Nuhertz Filter Solutions/Filter Light/Filter Free, MMICAD (Optotek). Source p.401 fn 19, p.422 §6.3.8B.

### Ch.7 Oscillators and Timers + Review (pp. 425-472; lines 25111-27190)
- Relaxation/logic oscillator edge integrity: scope the output at 40 ns/div, 5 V/div on each edge; "good" = single clean transition, no ~90-100 MHz burst; bad = ragged edges (Fig 7.3). Repeat with and without the 47 pF speed-up capacitor. (p.427 Fig 7.3)
- Schmitt/logic oscillator frequency vs supply: sweep V_DD over its range, plot f vs V_DD; expect ~proportional dependence for 74HC14 type; count cycle-to-cycle edge jitter on a scope (few % is typical). (p.427)
- 555 sawtooth undershoot: scope the timing capacitor at the discharge edge; pass if the ramp bottom does not drop below 1/3 Vcc; increase DIS series resistor until discharge takes >= 1/5/10 us (LMC555/ICL7555/bipolar). (p.430 fn.10)
- 555 supply glitch: probe supply current (or supply voltage at the pin) during output transitions; bipolar 555 up to 150 mA spikes; check for double output transitions. (p.429)
- Sine-oscillator purity: measure THD (distortion analyzer or FFT) vs frequency; plot THD (%) vs f_out over the tuning range; e.g., tracking SC filter target <= 0.1% over 100 Hz-10 kHz; Wien target 0.002%-0.0002%; identify dominant harmonic (2nd = JFET/asymmetry, 3rd = X7R caps). (p.436-438)
- Wien amplitude settling: step the supply or load and record amplitude envelope vs time; slow AGC (seconds) is expected; verify no cycle-by-cycle amplitude modulation. (p.437-438)
- LC/VCO spectral purity: spectrum analyzer around the carrier, 10 dB/div vertical, 200 kHz/div horizontal (Fig 7.30); compare sideband noise skirts and PLL reference spurs (e.g., 200 kHz) against a reference synthesizer; good = narrow skirt, no reference spurs (trap in place). (p.441-442)
- Varactor pulling vs amplitude: measure f_osc vs oscillation amplitude and vs dc tuning bias; plot f vs V_tune over the range; good = monotonic curve, minimal amplitude sensitivity with bias > ~1 V. (p.441)
- Crystal characterization: vector impedance analyzer (e.g., HP4192A: 0.01 ohm-200 kohm, 1 Hz resolution, to 13 MHz): sweep through fs and fa; record fs, R1 (= abs(Z) at fs), C0, fa, fp with a known CL; compute C1, L1; SPICE-plot abs(Z) and phase vs frequency offset for CL = 0-30 pF (Fig 7.35-7.37). (p.444-446)
- Crystal oscillator start-up/robustness: run the full circuit in SPICE with the crystal model including worst-case R1 (e.g., 50 ohm instead of 4.7 ohm) and confirm start-up and correct mode; on hardware, test start-up and frequency over the expected V_DD and temperature range with crystals from each approved vendor (IC vendors' capacitor values are "not optimized"). (p.445-446, p.449)
- Frequency vs load capacitance: measure f_osc with the actual board strays; compare to crystal's stamped frequency at its specified CL; adjust a load capacitor (e.g., 32 pF trimmer position) to center. (p.445-446)
- Micropower oscillator supply current: plot total supply current vs V_DD (0.8-3.3 V) for first stage and full chain, with and without supply-lead resistors (Fig 7.41); good = sub-uA oscillator core, few uA total. (p.448-449)
- Timing jitter: trigger the scope on one edge and view a later edge with persistence (or measure period histogram); compare to dt = v_n/S; for long-term stability plot Allan deviation vs averaging time. (p.457 fn.54)
- Step-triggered pulse circuits: bench-vs-SPICE overlay at 20 ns/div, 1 V/div including 8 pF probe capacitance at probed nodes (Fig 7.53); check delay, saturation voltage, overshoot. (p.458-459)
- One-shot width qualification: measure t_w at R = 10 k, C = 100 nF to confirm the vendor's K; sweep V+ (Fig 7.60 K vs supply), temperature (0-50 degC), and duty cycle up to 99.98%; check minimum trigger width and retrigger recovery. (p.463-464)
- Pulse limiter functional test: command a stuck-on (long) input pulse and a burst at excessive repetition rate; verify output width clamps at T1 and repetition is blocked until T2 after the last trigger. (p.465)
- Long timers: measure oscillator frequency and scale by the count (e.g., 2^13) rather than waiting out the full interval; trim to target; verify power-on reset sequencing (TimerBlox RST vs 1.7 ms init). (p.466-467)

### Ch.8 §8.1-§8.8: noise fundamentals, BJT/JFET low-noise design (pp. 473-521; lines 27191-30489)
- Johnson-noise sanity plot: y = en (nV/sqrt(Hz), log) and in (pA/sqrt(Hz), log) vs x = R (ohm, log, 1 ohm-100M); en slope +1/2, in slope -1/2; crossing check: 1k -> 4.06 nV/sqrt(Hz) at 25 degC (p.475 Fig. 8.1, table).
- 1/f corner plot: y = en (log) vs x = f (Hz, log, 1 Hz-10 kHz); fit en(f) = en(white)*sqrt(1 + fc/f); good = measured slope -1/2 below fc, flat above; report fc (p.476 Fig. 8.3, eq 8.8).
- Burst-noise screen: device as G = 100 noninverting amp, grounded input, output bandpass 0.1 Hz-3 kHz (6 dB/octave), scope V 1 mV/div (10 uV/div RTI), H 10 ms/div; pass = no two-level telegraph steps; also compare RTI noise spectrum with a known-quiet sample of same lot (p.477 Fig. 8.5, 8.6).
- Noise-vs-source-resistance chart: y = effective input noise density (V/sqrt(Hz)) vs x = Rs (log), with constant-NF contour lines; used to see which of en or in dominates at the actual Rs (p.480 Fig. 8.7).
- BJT en vs Ic plot: y = en (nV/sqrt(Hz), log) vs x = Ic (1 uA-100 mA, log), family of spot frequencies and rbb' values; good = slope -1/2 at low Ic flattening at sqrt(4kT*rbb'); low-f curves rise again at high Ic (base-current noise through rbb') (p.482 Fig. 8.11, 8.12; model vs measured at four currents for six BJTs).
- BJT in vs f plot: y = in (pA/sqrt(Hz)) vs x = f (1 Hz-100 kHz), family of Ic; good = flat sqrt(2qIb) mid-band, 1/sqrt(f) rise below fci (fci rising with Ic), rise ~f approaching fT (p.484-485 Fig. 8.13-8.15).
- en spectra at fixed Ic = 10 mA for candidate transistors (y = en, x = 1 Hz-100 kHz) to expose high-rbb' parts (p.486 Fig. 8.17); en spectra at five Ic for one part to pick Ic (ZTX851, Fig. 8.18).
- Current-source shot-noise test (Fig. 8.20C): ~10 uA BJT current source, 1 Mohm collector load, large emitter resistor (1 M vs re 2.5 k); measure noise density at collector (expect ~181-190 nV/sqrt(Hz) if quiet, ~1790 if shot noise present) and at emitter (~4.8 nV/sqrt(Hz)); then bypass B-E and confirm collector rises to full shot noise (~1664-1679 nV/sqrt(Hz)) (p.487-488).
- NF-contour reduction plots: NF vs Ic (one curve per Rs) and NF vs Rs (one curve per Ic) at 1 kHz from the datasheet contours; dashed NF = 3 dB line; then en vs Ic (from Rs = 50 ohm NF) and en vs f (per decade Ic) (p.489-491 Fig. 8.22-8.26).
- Total-noise-vs-source-resistance chart (the book's main design tool): x = Rs (1 ohm-100 Mohm, log), y = noise density (log); traces: en (flat), in*Rs (45 deg), sqrt(4kTRs) (= NF 3 dB line), total ea passing 3 dB above the en/in*Rs crossing; one family per Ic (decades 1 uA-10 mA) and per candidate part; read best NF at Rn and the Rs window where NF < 3 dB (2N5087 @ 10 uA, 1 kHz: 0.65 dB at 42k; < 3 dB from 2k to 1M) (p.494-504 Fig. 8.32, 8.33, 8.41; BJT vs JFET Fig. 8.53). For reactive sources omit the 4kTRs region (p.505).
- NF vs frequency for chosen (Ic, Rs) pairs (y = NF dB, x = 10 Hz-100 kHz) to confirm the chosen Ic at the lowest signal frequency (p.493 Fig. 8.30).
- Beta vs Ic at VCE = 5 V (x = 1 uA-50 mA log, y = beta) for every candidate BJT; then in = sqrt(2*q*Ic/beta) at the chosen Ic (p.503 Fig. 8.39).
- Batch LF-noise screen for unspecified parts: en spectra of several samples from one batch (and from several manufacturers of the "same" part), y = nV/sqrt(Hz), x = 1 Hz-100 kHz; reject lots with > 10 dB LF spread or plateaus (p.503 Fig. 8.40; p.517 Fig. 8.51).
- Vbe histogram of a batch (e.g. 100 pcs) before omitting emitter ballast in paralleled BJT arrays (p.508 Fig. 8.44).
- ZTX851-type en spectra across Ic (down to 4 Hz) to find the Ic "sweet spot" before LF base-current flicker rises (~5 mA) (p.507 Fig. 8.43).
- JFET en vs gm scatter (en measured well above the 1/f corner vs measured gm) with the 2/(3gm) Johnson line as lower bound; good = points on or within 10-30 % above the line (p.509 Fig. 8.46).
- JFET en vs f at VDS = 5 V, several Id (100 uA, 1 mA, 5-10 mA) to reveal 1/f corners and "roller-coaster" plateaus; integrate to rms over 1 kHz and 10 kHz (p.510-518 Fig. 8.47, 8.52).
- Transistor-noise test fixture: DUT grounded-emitter with settable Ic, VCE, known gain; output noise spectrum with input bypassed (en) and with series R (in) (p.508 §8.5.9D, details §8.12.2).
- Transformerless ribbon preamp verification: en spectrum 10 Hz-10 kHz for each array option; pass = en < 0.1 nV/sqrt(Hz) at 100 Hz and 1 kHz (p.508 Fig. 8.45).
- MOSFET vs JFET vs BJT en spectra (quietest and jellybean of each) to justify device-family choice below 1 MHz (p.520 Fig. 8.54).

### Ch.8 §8.9-§8.10: op-amp circuit noise, low-noise op-amp selection, signal transformers (pp. 521-537; lines 30490-33317)
- Total noise vs source resistance (Fig. 8.58 style): x = Rs log 10 ohm to 100 Mohm (extend to 10 Gohm for FET parts), y = en_tot (nV/rtHz) log, at 1 kHz AND at the lowest operating frequency; overlay the Johnson-noise line sqrt(4kTRs) and each candidate's en_tot curve; good = selected part's curve lies closest to the Johnson line at the design Rs (the op-amp's own contribution negligible). Source: p.526, p.533 (AOE-2549, AOE-2580).
- Noise spectra: x = f log 1 Hz to 1 MHz, y = en (nV/rtHz) and in (pA/rtHz or fA/rtHz) log, from datasheet or measurement; mark the en and in 1/f corners and any HF peaking (LT1028: 150-600 kHz); good = en and in below budget over the whole signal band. Source: p.526-527 Figs. 8.60-8.61 (AOE-2546, AOE-2552).
- Integrated noise vs bandwidth (Fig. 8.63 style): x = upper band edge f log 1 Hz-100 kHz, y = Vn (uV rms) log, computed from en and fc via eq. 8.59; good = curve below the requirement at the design bandwidth; compare BJT/JFET/CMOS/chopper candidates. Source: p.531 (AOE-2566, AOE-2567).
- 0.1-10 Hz peak-to-peak measurement: 10 s oscilloscope record of the output through a 0.1-10 Hz bandpass (1- or 2-pole RC, or 2-3-pole Butterworth), referred to input by the gain; pass = Vpp <= spec and consistent with 6 x the computed brick-wall rms. Source: p.532 Fig. 8.64 (AOE-2569).
- en vs Iq scatter for part selection (Fig. 8.57): x = Iq (mA) log, y = en at 1 kHz log; accept candidates within 2x of the lower envelope at the available Iq. Source: p.525-526 (AOE-2547, AOE-2548).
- en vs Cin scatter / FOM ranking (Fig. 8.65): x = Cin (pF), y = en; compute en*(Cin+Cext) for the actual external capacitance; good = lowest FOM part chosen. Source: p.532 (AOE-2571, AOE-2572).
- PSRR per rail: inject a small sine on one rail at a time, sweep 10 Hz-1 MHz, measure output (or read datasheet curves); plot PSRR (dB) vs f for +V and -V; good = required attenuation met on BOTH rails across the band; if not, add RC filter or capacitance multiplier and re-measure. Source: p.533 (AOE-2575, AOE-2576).
- Input bias current vs common-mode voltage and temperature: sweep Vcm rail to rail and Tj over the operating range (include self-heating and in-box temperature); good = worst-case Ib (and derived in) within budget; watch the rise near the rails for bias-cancelled RRI parts. Source: p.527, p.530 (AOE-2556, AOE-2563, AOE-2564).
- Hybrid (discrete front end + op-amp) stability: simulate loop gain and phase at the intended closed-loop gain (AC analysis) and run a small-signal step; good = adequate phase margin (second-stage OPA277 alone ~60 deg) and no ringing at Gcl >= front-end gain; otherwise add series R+C across the op-amp inputs. Source: p.534-535 (AOE-2585).
- Transformer-coupled input: measure frequency response (+/-0.15 dB 20 Hz-20 kHz, -3 dB points), THD at 20 Hz and 1 kHz, CMR at 60 Hz, and NF vs frequency with the real source impedance; good = meets the JT-115K-E-class benchmark and NF target. Source: p.535-536 (AOE-2595, AOE-2596).
- Transformer-feedback amplifier LF stability: simulate/measure loop gain near the coupling-capacitor and magnetizing-inductance breakpoints; good = no LF peaking or motorboating, i.e. the C2 breakpoint well below the transformer's. Source: p.537 (AOE-2603).

### Ch.8 §8.11-§8.16 + Review: transimpedance amplifiers, noise measurement, supply noise, interference/shielding/grounding (pp. 537-593; lines 33318-35540)
- TIA noise and gain chart (p.540-541 Fig. 8.73): three stacked log-log plots vs frequency: (1) transimpedance gain Vout/Iin (flat to fc, then limited by open-loop gain); (2) noise gain Vout/en (rising enC slope, flat above fc, then limited by GOL); (3) input-referred noise current = RSS(en*omega*Cin, sqrt(4kT/Rf)) shaped by the rolloff. Sweep Cin over the full expected range (e.g. 10 pF, 100 pF, 1 nF, 10 nF). Good: bandwidth ~unchanged across Cin up to the design maximum, no peaking beyond damping ratio ~0.7.
- Measure TIA output noise with an FFT spectrum analyzer (SR785 used), input capped by each test capacitance; also measure the op-amp's own en; overlay predicted curves. Expect en 1/f rise (about 3x at 100 Hz for OPA656) to add low-frequency excess (p.540 §8.11.5 Fig. 8.74).
- Plot input-referred noise current before and after the output lowpass (Fig. 8.76) at minimum and maximum Cin; pass = out-of-band noise region removed (p.543).
- Bootstrapped TIA comparison (p.548 Fig. 8.84): plot transimpedance gain and input-referred noise current vs frequency (1 kHz-10 MHz, log-log) for normal (1000 pF) vs bootstrapped (100 pF effective) input, with separate op-amp-en and JFET-en contributions; good = bandwidth up, enC noise down, gain unchanged.
- Five-TIA comparison with 1000 pF source (p.546 Fig. 8.81): transimpedance gain and input noise current vs frequency for single-stage, composite, hybrid-composite and bootstrapped designs; Rf Johnson floors marked (40 fA/rtHz for 10 Mohm, 29 fA/rtHz for 20 Mohm).
- TIA compensation adjustment (p.555 §8.11.13 Fig. 8.91): inject a clean nA square wave (0.1 V square through R1C1-compensated scaling resistor, or 0.5 Vpp 1 kHz triangle through 1 pF), with the real input cable attached; trim the compensation (R2) for best step response (no overshoot/ringing); a calibrated function generator (e.g. Agilent 33120A) gives clean triangle turning points.
- en/in measurement without a noise source (p.555 §8.12.1): (1) measure Gv with a sine in band; (2) shorted input, rms output es in known ENBW -> en; (3) resistor R across input, rms er -> in. Requires accurate rms voltmeter, known filter ENBW and gain vs frequency with actual source resistance.
- Noise-source (3 dB) method (p.556 §8.12.3): source impedance = intended Rs; raise calibrated noise until output rms rises by x1.414; repeat at several Rs to separate en and in.
- Y-factor (p.557): hot (room) and cold (77 K) 50 ohm terminations through a coax relay, RF power meter at fixed center frequency/bandwidth; Tn from Y = Ph/Pc.
- Noise-source verification (p.560 Fig. 8.95): measure the generator's output spectrum with two 800-point FFT spans stitched to cover 100,000:1; check flatness (white +-0.07 dB to 100 kHz), -3 dB/octave pink (+-0.25 dB 10 Hz-1 MHz in SPICE), and band-limited rms (4-pole Butterworth, 10 Hz-10 kHz).
- Integrated-noise curve (p.566 Fig. 8.104): plot vn vs upper cutoff f2 (log-log) from the datasheet en(f) with a fixed nonzero f1; flattening at low f2 reveals the 1/f region.
- Decade-band time traces (p.567 Fig. 8.106): record single-shot noise in successive decade bandpasses (0.1-1, 1-10, 10-100 Hz); ideal pink -> equal amplitude per decade, white -> sqrt(10) per decade; rising amplitude at the lowest decade reveals steeper-than-pink noise.
- Op-amp en and in spectra (p.568 Figs. 8.108-8.111): shielded box with filtered dc feedthroughs; FFT analyzer; x-axis 0.5 Hz-100 kHz log, y-axis nV/rtHz or fA/rtHz log; en with Rs = 0, in with Rs = 1-100 Mohm (Rs Johnson floor drawn as a reference line); auto-zero parts: flat LF but clock peaks; conventional: 1/f rise below fc.
- fA/rtHz current noise (p.572 Fig. 8.114): composite integrator output vn vs f should fall exactly as 1/f for white in; read in = vn*2*pi*f*Cf; the upper usable frequency is where en*omega*C takes over; calibrate against a part of known in (LTC1049 100 fA/rtHz, AD8628).
- Power-supply noise survey (p.580 Fig. 8.123): measure each supply's output noise spectrum (SR785) with a 100 ohm load; plot nV/rtHz vs frequency (log-log), note powerline tonals (dB above smoothed baseline at 120 Hz, uV rms in 1 Hz BW) and LF drift tail; a trickle-charged lead-acid battery sets the practical floor.
- Capacitance-multiplier effectiveness (p.579 Fig. 8.122): three spectra per supply, 100 ohm load: (a) supply alone, (b) with 10,000 uF across its terminals, (c) after the capacitance multiplier; pass = (c) near analyzer floor across the band of interest (40 dB or more improvement).
- Lock-in sanity test (p.578): LED modulated at ~1 kHz, phototransistor 6 ft away, time constant a few seconds; switching room lights (>50 dB signal swing) must not change the detected level; blocking the beam must.
- Inter-instrument ground noise (p.583 Fig. 8.126): measure the ac voltage between local grounds of the instruments to be connected (expect >= 100 mV at line frequency) before choosing single-ended vs differential/isolated links.

### Ch.9 §9.1-§9.5: linear regulators, heat and SOA, ac line to unregulated dc (pp. 594-636; lines 35530-38215)
- Regulator loop stability (discrete or IC): apply a load-current step (e.g. 10%-90% of rated) with the intended downstream bypass capacitance; plot Vout(t). Good = well-damped (no ringing/oscillation); for discrete designs, raise Cc until well damped, then double it (p.597 §9.1.1C). Sim: transient in ngspice with the real C_bypass(total) and its ESR.
- Current-limit verification: short the output (or sweep load resistance to zero); plot I_out vs R_load and pass-transistor dissipation; good = I_out clamps near Vbe/Rcl and dissipation within SOA (p.597 §9.1.1C).
- Crowbar verification: raise output (or simulate pass-transistor C-E short); confirm SCR fires at ~Vz + 0.6 V and the fuse clears (p.598).
- Low-line headroom: run at minimum line (105 Vac for 120 Vac nominal) and full load; scope the unregulated input and confirm the ripple trough stays above V_out + dropout (p.600 footnote 9).
- LDO stability across capacitor ESR: simulate (or bench) a load step at min and max load with the output capacitor's ESR swept across its min/max over temperature; plot Vout(t). Good = damped response everywhere inside the datasheet's stable region; watch for wrong/zero output or a raised dc output (LM2940 oscillating read 7.5 V on a 5 V rail) (p.617 §9.3.12F, Fig. 9.27).
- Load-step transient (Fig. 9.28-9.31): step the load (e.g. 2->4 A and 1->5 A on a 1 V/6 A rail) with a MOSFET-switched load; scope at 4 us/div (edge) and 400 us/div (full cycle). Good = mV-level dip (~4 mV on a good SMT layout vs ~40 mV on through-hole protoboard) and recovery to within a fraction of a mV (p.617-618).
- Dropout characterization: sweep V_in down through V_out + V_do at light and full load; plot V_out and ground-pin current vs V_in. Good = no ground-current explosion for battery designs (pnp LDOs rise ~10x) (p.616 §9.3.12D).
- Regulator output noise: measure rms noise in a stated band (10 Hz-100 kHz default) with and without the NR/ADJ capacitor; compare parts only on the same band and rms basis (p.615 Table 9.3 notes; p.619).
- Current-source compliance: plot I_out vs voltage across the 2-terminal source (Fig. 9.35, 9.37, 9.38, 9.40, 9.41) on an expanded current scale; good = flat current above the minimum compliance voltage (LM317 ~3 V, LT3092 ~1.2 V) (p.621-623).
- Thermal verification: at maximum ambient and full dissipation, measure case/heatsink temperature (thermocouple, IR probe with 1 mV/C output, or Tempilstik wax) and back-calculate Tj = T_case + P*R_thJC; good = Tj well below maximum (e.g. <= 100 C for a 125 C part) (p.624-625).
- Unregulated rectifier supply bench test (Fig. 9.51): with full resistive load and low/high line, record transformer secondary voltage and current, and ac-coupled output ripple (4 ms/div); measure line and secondary currents with a true-rms meter. Good = ripple <= design (0.8 Vpp measured vs 1 Vpp design), conduction angle ~60 deg per half-cycle, secondary Irms within rating (~2x I_dc), no leakage-inductance/recovery spikes with the RC snubber fitted (p.635 §9.5.4).
- SPICE model of the rectifier supply (Fig. 9.52): include transformer winding resistances, leakage and magnetizing inductances, rectifier model, capacitor ESR and snubber (fn 50 parameter set); compare waveforms with bench; expect the simulation to slightly underestimate conduction angle (p.635).
- Switcher vs linear noise (Fig. 9.53-9.54): measure at 50% load both across the supply output pins and at a load 50 cm away (0.1 V/div, 100 ns/div for spikes), and take an averaged spectrum 0-250 kHz at 3 kHz RBW to see the (dithered) switching frequency and harmonics. Good for a linear supply = ~0.5 mVpp 120 Hz; switchers show switching ripple (~6 mVpp at pins) and ground-current spikes (~150 mV at the remote load) that the layout/filtering must control (p.636).

## 5. Pitfalls, failure modes, review checklist
### Ch.5 §5.10-§5.14: choosing precision op-amps, auto-zero amplifiers, DMM front ends, difference amplifiers (pp. 319-356; lines 18822-21122)
- [ ] Budget uses max (not typical) Vos and Ib; typical-to-max Ib can be 200x for sub-10 pA parts (p.323, p.325).
- [ ] FET-input Ib evaluated at T_max (exponential leakage growth, Fig. 5.38, p.325).
- [ ] Input common-mode and output swing checked against the real rails (LT1007 needs 2 V headroom) (p.322).
- [ ] SHDN pin current and 25 us wake time included when using op-amp shutdown (LT6010) (p.322).
- [ ] Noise evaluated with en at the top of the band, not at 1 kHz, for wideband/TIA circuits (p.324).
- [ ] Low-frequency noise compared using the 1/f corner (LT1012 vs OPA277 vs TLC2272) (p.324).
- [ ] Source resistance well below Zn = en/in (LT1028: <= 300 ohm) (p.326).
- [ ] Parenthesized in values not trusted (5x-100x low) (p.321 note o).
- [ ] MOSFET-input op-amps not left with large dc differential input in long-life precision service (p.322 fn 32).
- [ ] RRIO op-amp offset vs Vcm reviewed on the datasheet (p.323).
- [ ] Bias-current-cancelled BJT op-amp current noise estimated from the uncancelled base current (x sqrt(2)), not net Ib (p.327).
- [ ] Datasheet in values for AZ and bias-cancelled parts treated as suspect (LT1012 10x, AD8628A 30x, MCP6V06 ~280-400x) (p.327-328, p.336).
- [ ] Op-amp input kept out of its high-Ib/degraded-Vos regions near the rails (LT1677: bottom 1.4 V, top 0.7 V) (p.327 fn 40).
- [ ] CMRR taken at the signal frequency and over the used Vcm range (LMC6482/OPA277/AD8622 ~80 dB at 10 kHz) (p.328).
- [ ] ac PSRR checked on the worse rail; low-level stages have RC supply filters (p.328).
- [ ] Decompensated op-amp run only above its minimum stable gain (THS4021 >10) (p.328-329).
- [ ] Distortion data compared only at matched level/load/gain/filter conditions (p.330).
- [ ] JFET op-amp non-inverting stage at high frequency has matched source impedances or is converted to inverting (OPA1641: 20 -> 100 ppm at 100 kHz with 600 ohm) (p.330).
- [ ] Capacitive-load driver sized from I = C*dV/dt and checked against its SOA (PB51: 130 V drop at 2 A for 100 ms) (p.332).
- [ ] DAC buffer: Ib*Rout_DAC and Vos below 1 LSB (DAC8831: 12 nA, 76 uV) (p.332).
- [ ] Piezo loop bandwidth <= ~20% of mechanical resonance; series RC across output; up to 100 uF bypass at ampere-level currents (p.332-333).
- [ ] Auto-zero op-amp not used where its output can saturate unless clamped or a fast-recovery part (OPA378 4 us, OPA734 8 us) is chosen (p.338).
- [ ] Auto-zero clock frequency and harmonics kept away from the signal band and its IM products, or a spread-spectrum-clock part used (p.336).
- [ ] Auto-zero output RC-filtered to a few hundred Hz in dc applications (p.337).
- [ ] Auto-zero chosen only when output bandwidth is very low (below ~1 Hz) or offset/drift truly requires it; broadband noise ~50-100 nV/sqrt(Hz) accepted (p.337-339).
- [ ] Headline AZ specs checked for their Vcm conditions (OPA335: CMRR only for Vcm <= V+ - 1.5 V; Vos at Vcm = Vs/2) (p.340).
- [ ] Thermal EMF: symmetrical wiring, isothermal junction pairs, no drafts; no Kovar/Cu-oxide junctions in sub-uV paths (p.340-341).
- [ ] Self-heating of ~1 mA AZ parts considered at nV/degC levels (p.339).
- [ ] Ground-return currents at nV-level nodes cancelled by a matched opposite current (p.345).
- [ ] Critical parts whose datasheet worst case does not meet the product spec are screened, with a lifetime-buy decision recorded (p.347).
- [ ] Integrated difference amp driven from a low-Z source; CMRR spec assumed only at Rs = 0 (p.352).
- [ ] Difference amp not used for G > 10; in-amp used instead (p.354).
- [ ] CMRR-induced input error with large Vcm computed at dc and at line frequency using min CMRR (AD629B: 10 mV at 200 V; INA117: 80 mV at 160 V pk, 60 Hz) (p.354-355).
- [ ] Mirror-image placement of difference-amp components and balanced common-mode impedances for HF CMRR (p.355).
- [ ] Low-side shunt read with 4-wire Kelvin connection (1 mohm connection = 10% error on a 10 mohm shunt) (p.350).
- [ ] High-side sensing with a difference amp only at low current; CMRR error drives shunt voltage (and power) up (p.350).
- [ ] Twisted-pair analog links terminated in 100 ohm and equalized for cable loss (p.352).

### Ch.5 §5.15-§5.17 + Review: instrumentation and fully differential amplifiers (pp. 356-390; lines 21123-23445)
- [ ] IA run at G = 1 delivers only difference-amp CMRR and noise; datasheet best-case CMRR/en are at high gain (p.358; AOE-2128).
- [ ] IA front-end amps clip before the output shows it: check Vcm +/- 0.5*Vdiff*(1 + 2Rf/Rg) (p.358; AOE-2131).
- [ ] Unbalanced source impedances with input/cable capacitance destroy CMRR even at 60 Hz (p.357 fn70, p.358-359; AOE-2124, AOE-2132).
- [ ] Guard derived from Rg pins may be offset from the inputs: fine for capacitance, poor for leakage (p.359; AOE-2133).
- [ ] External offset trim on a laser-trimmed op-amp has ~50x too much range and is unstable (p.361; AOE-2142).
- [ ] Mux switch on-resistance inside the gain path of a PGIA (p.361; AOE-2143).
- [ ] External rail clamps without a series resistor to the op-amp input share fault current with the internal diodes (p.363; AOE-2150).
- [ ] Input series resistor not rated for 500 V / 250 W surge energy (p.362 fn80; AOE-2148).
- [ ] Op-amp input phase reversal during overdrive (p.361 fn79; p.384 fn110: LM324/358 -> use LT1013/1014) (AOE-2141, AOE-2217).
- [ ] Floating source (thermocouple, transformer, ac-coupled) with no bias-return resistor: INA saturates (p.366; AOE-2164).
- [ ] REF pin driven from a resistive divider or trimmer instead of a precision op-amp: CMRR collapse (p.366; AOE-2161).
- [ ] INA internal-node saturation near the rails at low gain even within the spec'd CM range (AD623: 1.0 V max output at Vcm = 0, G = 10) (p.366; AOE-2165).
- [ ] Configuration-E INA damaged by > +/-0.5 V differential input; parts with back-to-back input diodes damaged by input current (p.368 fn85; AOE-2172).
- [ ] Configuration-F INA with REF grounded does not operate (p.368; AOE-2173).
- [ ] Flying-capacitor INA aliases at half the chopping frequency and settles slowly (p.370; AOE-2177).
- [ ] Auto-zero INA/op-amp switching noise often not shown on datasheets; slow saturation recovery (ms) (p.370 fn90, p.389-390; AOE-2179, AOE-2238).
- [ ] TI/Burr-Brown INAs listed as type A may actually be type C; datasheets silent on topology (p.369 fn86).
- [ ] BJT-input amplifiers rectify RF (forward-biased junctions): offset shifts when cables are handled (p.366, p.369 fn87; AOE-2160, AOE-2175).
- [ ] Differential output made with an inverter after the INA output: gain error from inverter resistor mismatch (p.373; AOE-2184).
- [ ] ADC driver on different/higher rails than the ADC: clamp conduction, SCR latchup, supply sequencing (p.374, p.380-381; AOE-2186, AOE-2206).
- [ ] FDA Vocm pin left floating without a bypass capacitor, or driven from a high-impedance ADC reference that the pin's tens-of-kohm input loads (p.374, 378, 383; AOE-2190, AOE-2214).
- [ ] FDA used single-ended: output CM error / G dwarfs the input offset spec (LMP7312: +/-20 mV out -> up to +/-200 mV RTI) (p.377-378; AOE-2196).
- [ ] Fixed-gain FDA offset = internal mismatch x Vocm (1% x 1.5 V = 15 mV) (p.384; AOE-2219).
- [ ] Datasheet "bandwidth" is at G = 1 with peaking (>= 1.5 x GBW); 0.1 dB bandwidth can be 7% of -3 dB bandwidth (p.385; AOE-2224, AOE-2227).
- [ ] Large Rf/Rg in a fast FDA: peaking, lost bandwidth, bias-current offset, noise (THS4521 100k: BW/10, offset x25, noise x16) (p.378-379; AOE-2199).
- [ ] Dual/quad FDA packages peak from lead-frame parasitics with external resistors (p.378; AOE-2202).
- [ ] HF RRO FDA distortion climbs within ~400 mV of the rails (p.383; AOE-2216).
- [ ] NRI FDA summing junction driven more than 0.2 V below -Vee: output polarity reversal (p.384; AOE-2217).
- [ ] Current-noise 1/f corners near 1 MHz and CFB current noise ~10x VFB (p.386-387; AOE-2231, AOE-2233).
- [ ] Headline distortion figures collapse well below the bandwidth (ADA4932: x10 worse by 50 MHz) (p.386; AOE-2229).
- [ ] PGIA/INA CMRR specified only at 60 Hz and without source imbalance (p.371 note x, Table 5.9 note d; AOE-2182).
- [ ] Transformer-coupled ADC drive offers no overdrive protection (p.382; AOE-2210).
- [ ] Buying a 0.05% reference when the driver gain error is 0.6% (p.374 fn97; AOE-2188).
- [ ] Capacitors in integrators/S&H: leakage (exponential decay) and dielectric absorption; analog switches: leakage and charge injection (p.388 Review ¶E).
- [ ] FET-input bias current rises dramatically with temperature, possibly above a bias-cancelled bipolar part (p.389 Review ¶F; AOE-2236).
- [ ] RRI op-amp Ib and Vos step at the input-stage crossover voltage (p.389 Review ¶H; AOE-2238).
- [ ] Table 5.8 gain equations for INA217/THAT1510 garbled in this extraction: verify on datasheets before use.

### Ch.6 Filters + Review (pp. 391-424; lines 23446-25110)
- [ ] RC cascades used where a sharp knee is required (soft knee regardless of order). (p.391, p.423)
- [ ] Buffered RC cascade: overall corner lower than each section's corner by sqrt(2^(1/n) - 1). (p.391 fn 1)
- [ ] RC supply filter relying on a single big electrolytic: ESR sets an attenuation floor; split into sections. (p.392-393)
- [ ] Series-LC sine filter driven by very fast edges without an RC prefilter: notches from inductor self-capacitance. (p.394)
- [ ] Anti-alias filter checked only at fs/2 but not at fs - f_pass_edge (worst-case alias onto band edge). (p.395-396)
- [ ] Anti-alias corner not tracking a variable sample rate. (p.412)
- [ ] Even-order Chebyshev: dc gain sits at the bottom of the ripple band (not at the 0 dB peak). (p.400)
- [ ] Chebyshev/elliptic chosen where step response matters: largest overshoot and slowest settling (Table 6.1: 8-pole 2 dB Cheb overshoot 34%, settling to 0.1% 24.8/fc). (p.406)
- [ ] Elliptic stopband does not keep falling; check attenuation at all stopband frequencies, not only the ultimate slope. (p.403)
- [ ] Bessel expected to give a sharp transition by adding poles (it does not). (p.403)
- [ ] VCVS section gain K >= 3 (oscillation) or near 3 (extreme Q sensitivity). (p.410)
- [ ] VCVS with R below ~10k: op-amp output impedance upsets response. (p.408)
- [ ] VCVS stopband floor from op-amp rising output impedance (input couples via R1-C1). (p.413-414)
- [ ] VCVS sections cascaded with high-gain section first (clipping). (p.408)
- [ ] Highpass Bessel/Chebyshev built without inverting cn. (p.409)
- [ ] Op-amp fT below ~50*Q^2*fc for VCVS, or below 10-20*G*f0 for biquad. (p.410, 412)
- [ ] Biquad/state-variable resistors below ~5k loading op-amps. (p.412)
- [ ] Sallen-Key component labels: C1/C2 swapped between AoE/Sallen-Key and TI (Karki) notation. (p.410 fn 28)
- [ ] High-Q bandpass built from cascaded uncoupled LP+HP sections (too sensitive). (p.409)
- [ ] Gyrator used to replace a floating inductor (most gyrators are ground-referenced). (p.398 fn 11)
- [ ] GIC inductor used above a few percent of op-amp fT; peaking not damped. (p.398)
- [ ] Twin-T "activated" by bootstrap expecting a deeper notch (null gets shallower); twin-T needs stable low-TC parts and one trim. (p.414)
- [ ] Bridged-differentiator bootstrapped too close to unity: high-side peak, lower ultimate attenuation. (p.415)
- [ ] Allpass with small R: low input impedance at HF. (p.415)
- [ ] Filter capacitors of electrolytic, tantalum or high-K ceramic type; high-K has large voltage coefficient; electrolytic has dielectric absorption. (p.416, 422)
- [ ] Magnetic-core inductors in linearity-critical LC filters (use good-linearity cores; air core to ~1 mH). (p.422)
- [ ] Digital pot used as an absolute resistor (+/-20%) rather than as a ratio divider. (p.412-413)
- [ ] Analog-switch R_on ignored when switching filter resistors. (p.413)
- [ ] Switched-capacitor filter without input anti-alias prefilter near f_clk (aliased energy is unrecoverable). (p.417-418)
- [ ] SC clock feedthrough (10-25 mV) not filtered; SC used as highpass with in-band clock artifacts. (p.417-418)
- [ ] SC dynamic range (80-90 dB) and distortion near rails not budgeted. (p.418)
- [ ] LTC1062-type external clock not rail-to-rail or unprotected (add small series resistor). (p.418 Fig. 6.46)
- [ ] FIR coefficients truncated without a window (stopband bumps); FIR/IIR word length too short for the claimed stopband; IIR idle tones/instability. (p.420-421)
- [ ] One-pole IIR: book text's "f3dB = fs/20" is inconsistent with tau = 20 samples (b = 0.95123 -> f3dB = fs/(40*pi)); compute from b. (p.420)
- [ ] Very sharp LC bandpass: huge nonlinear in-band phase (495-1270 deg) and ~9 dB insertion loss in the Fig. 6.5 example. (p.393-394)
- [ ] LC filter OCR note: Fig. 6.9 L2 printed "101mH" - value is 101 uH. (p.396)

### Ch.7 Oscillators and Timers + Review (pp. 425-472; lines 25111-27190)
- [ ] CMOS-inverter RC oscillator without a ~47 pF speed-up capacitor: parasitic ~100 MHz bursts at every edge (p.426-427 Fig 7.3).
- [ ] Schmitt-trigger (74HC14) RC oscillator specified for accurate frequency: hysteresis only guaranteed 0.5-1.5 V, >= 50% spread (p.427).
- [ ] Bipolar 555 without a hefty local bypass capacitor: 150 mA transition spikes, double output transitions (p.429).
- [ ] 555 astable expected to give 50% duty with RA/RB network: impossible, always > 50% (p.429).
- [ ] CMOS 555 asked to source/sink bipolar-555 load currents: CMOS parts have much weaker outputs (p.429).
- [ ] 555 sawtooth with small C and no discharge-slowing resistor: ramp undershoots below 1/3 Vcc (p.430).
- [ ] Unbuffered 555 timing-capacitor node used as a waveform output (p.430, p.432).
- [ ] Constant-current ramp with supply-tracking thresholds on a battery: frequency drifts with supply; use ratiometric current (p.431).
- [ ] Programmable oscillator resolution mistaken for accuracy (DS1085 +/-1%) (p.434).
- [ ] Fixed lowpass used to make sine from a square wave whose frequency changes more than ~+/-25% (p.436).
- [ ] Wien AGC time constant comparable to one period: distortion from in-cycle gain modulation (p.437).
- [ ] X7R/ceramic capacitors in a low-distortion oscillator: 100-fold THD increase (p.438).
- [ ] JFET variable resistor with > 100 mV swing or without Vds/2 gate linearization (p.438).
- [ ] Wien JFET gain element without guaranteed start-up (check g_m,min vs required R_on) (p.438).
- [ ] LTC1799/TimerBlox programming resistor or DIV divider outside the datasheet's recommended region, or DIV divider not 1% resistors (p.432-433).
- [ ] RC phase-shift oscillator output taken from the clipped amplifier instead of the last RC section (p.439).
- [ ] Varactor-tuned oscillator with large oscillation amplitude or dc bias below ~1 V (distortion, amplitude-dependent frequency) (p.441).
- [ ] PLL VCO tuning line without a trap/filter at the phase-detector reference frequency (reference spurs) (p.441 fn.35).
- [ ] Apparent "oscillation" that is really 50/60/120 Hz pickup, switcher noise (20 kHz-1 MHz), broadcast RF, or scope aliasing (p.442).
- [ ] Circuit that behaves differently when probed or touched: suspect parasitic oscillation; add base stopper resistor (p.442).
- [ ] Series-cut crystal used in a parallel-mode (Pierce/inverter) oscillator, or parallel-cut crystal in a series-resonant input, or wrong CL (p.444-445, p.449).
- [ ] Crystal load capacitors chosen without subtracting pin and trace strays; strays drifting > 0.002 pF where 0.1 ppm is needed (p.445).
- [ ] Oscillator margin verified only with a low-ESR sample crystal (4.7 ohm) instead of the specified maximum (50 ohm): may not start or may run at an unrelated frequency (p.446, p.449).
- [ ] Crystal mounted far from the IC's XTAL pins; untested second-source crystal vendor (p.449).
- [ ] Ceramic resonator used where better than ~0.3% initial / 0.2%-1% drift is required (p.450).
- [ ] Integrator/differentiator used to make a "quadrature" pair: amplitude varies with frequency (p.453).
- [ ] Low-voltage zener (soft knee) used as a quadrature-oscillator limiter; no capacitor across zener/reference (p.454).
- [ ] DDS output used without reconstruction filter, or filter corner incompatible with required tuning span (p.452).
- [ ] Slow edges fed to a threshold/clock input: jitter = noise/slew (p.457).
- [ ] RC-differentiator + transistor pulse generator used for accurate timing (width depends on threshold) (p.458).
- [ ] Falling-edge BJT pulse circuit driven with > ~6 V step (B-E reverse breakdown) (p.458).
- [ ] 555 monostable triggered with a pulse longer than the output pulse (p.460).
- [ ] '123 one-shot with RESET used for power-up suppression while both trigger inputs are asserted (fires on reset release); unused one-shot inputs left in the wrong state (p.462).
- [ ] 74HC123 (or other '123) sourced from multiple vendors with K = 1.0 vs 0.45 (2.2x width difference) (p.463).
- [ ] Short one-shot pulse computed without t_min (p.463 fn.61).
- [ ] One-shot with timing C ~1000 pF or less on a part whose lower threshold is not near ground (unstable width) (p.463).
- [ ] Long one-shot timing with high-k ceramic (50%+ C variation) or leaky electrolytic; no diode across R for abrupt power-down (p.463-464).
- [ ] One-shot sharing a noisy V+ with logic; no private RC-filtered supply (p.464).
- [ ] One-shot or flip-flop output driving a cable directly (p.464).
- [ ] Design built from chains of one-shots instead of synchronous clocked delays (p.465).
- [ ] Software/MCU directly driving overdriven pulsed loads (LEDs at 10x continuous current) with no hardware pulse-width/duty limiter (p.465).
- [ ] TimerBlox RST asserted at end of ~1.7 ms initialization: first full timing cycle skipped (p.467 fn.63).
- [ ] Logic VCO (74LS624 family) assumed accurate/linear: likely only ~+/-20% (p.470).
- [ ] Programmable timer with output flip-flop but no set/clear inputs (MC14541) where external control of the state is needed (p.468).

### Ch.8 §8.1-§8.8: noise fundamentals, BJT/JFET low-noise design (pp. 473-521; lines 27191-30489)
- [ ] Rms noise amplitudes were added linearly instead of root-sum-square (p.479 §8.2.1).
- [ ] SNR quoted without bandwidth and center frequency (p.479 §8.2.2).
- [ ] Series resistor added to source "to improve NF" (it degrades SNR) (p.480 §8.2.3).
- [ ] Datasheet NF used at non-optimum Rs/Ic without recomputation (p.480 §8.2.3).
- [ ] NF formula with factor 4 applied to a matched 50-ohm RF system (drop the 4) or without-4 applied to a high-Zin amp (p.480 §8.2.3).
- [ ] Carbon-composition resistor carrying dc in a low-noise path (excess noise up to 3.0 uV/V/decade) (p.476 §8.1.3).
- [ ] Shot-noise formula applied to metallic conductor current or a transistor current source (overestimate) (p.476 §8.1.2).
- [ ] White-noise sqrt(B) scaling used on a 1/f-dominated band (must integrate) (p.478 §8.1.5).
- [ ] Cellphone (periodic "galloping" transmissions even when idle), switching regulators, motors, radio/TV stations considered as interference sources near sensitive front ends (p.478 §8.1.6).
- [ ] Microphonic (vibration/sound-sensitive) circuits, detectors, and cables identified in sensitive measurements (p.478 §8.1.6).
- [ ] Input blocking capacitor sized only for the LF rolloff, not for in*Xc noise at the lowest frequency (p.484 §8.3.2).
- [ ] Low-rbb' transistor chosen but operated at low Ic where re dominates (no benefit) (p.482 §8.3.1).
- [ ] BJT noise model (rbb'-only) trusted below 100 uA without the 10-20 % allowance (p.482 Fig. 8.12).
- [ ] Undegenerated current mirror assumed to be shot-noise-free (only a large-Re degenerated source is quiet) (p.489 §8.3.5).
- [ ] Emitter of a current source bypassed to ground/base (restores full shot noise) (p.488 §8.3.5).
- [ ] Emitter-follower output assumed noiseless (carries re*sqrt(2qIc)) (p.487 §8.3.5).
- [ ] NF below 1 dB read off a contour plot that has no sub-1 dB contours (p.489 §8.4.1).
- [ ] Typical NF (e.g. 0.2 dB) used when max is 3 dB (p.489 footnote 30).
- [ ] 50-ohm source driven directly into a high-noise-resistance (JFET) amp (NF 15 dB) instead of via a step-up signal transformer (p.492 §8.4.6).
- [ ] Unity-gain follower used as the "low-noise" first stage (it just passes the noise problem to the next stage) (p.493 §8.5.1).
- [ ] Lowest-en BJT picked from a table and run at 10 mA with a 600 ohm source: base-current noise (in*Rs = 2.3 nV/sqrt(Hz)) swamps the 0.2 nV/sqrt(Hz) en (p.503 §8.5.8).
- [ ] Beta assumed at datasheet test current (often mA) while the circuit runs at uA; pnp beta falloff at low current (2N3906: 3x over four decades) (p.502 §8.5.8).
- [ ] Top beta grade chosen by default (worse en, lower VCEO, lower VA, poor availability) (p.502 footnote 42).
- [ ] pnp current-mirror load without degeneration on an npn differential pair (pnp VA as low as 30 V kills gain) (p.502 §8.5.8).
- [ ] Noise-vs-Rs chart including 4kTRs used for a capacitive (reactive) sensor (p.505 §8.5.8).
- [ ] Low-rbb' (large-die) BJT used with a high-impedance source at low Ic (high Cob degrades performance) (p.505 footnote 47).
- [ ] Unspecified noise of a part number assumed equal to one sample batch; same number from other makers (2N5486: up to 15x LF noise) or other lots (>10 dB) (p.503, p.510-511, p.517 Fig. 8.40, 8.51).
- [ ] Paralleled BJTs without emitter ballast and without a measured Vbe-match check; or paralleled JFETs assumed matched (p.508 §8.5.9C).
- [ ] Open-loop class-A low-noise stage fed from a noisy rail (appears 1:1 at the output) (p.508 §8.5.9C).
- [ ] JFET run so hot that gate leakage (and its flicker/shot noise), drift and CMRR degrade for a mere Id^(-1/4) en gain (p.509 §8.6.1).
- [ ] JFETs paralleled past the point where input capacitance exceeds the source capacitance (p.510 §8.6.1).
- [ ] JFET datasheet en at 10 kHz+ (or RF) used for an audio/LF design (p.511 §8.6.1A).
- [ ] Worst-case JFET gate leakage (1 nA) used for in when typical is 1-10 pA, or conversely hot/high-VDG leakage ignored (n-channel leakage rises rapidly at high VDG/Id) (p.511 §8.6.2).
- [ ] Non-cascoded common-source JFET feeding an RF-tuned input (Miller-induced input resistance lowers Q) (p.511-512 §8.6.2).
- [ ] JFET stage with VDS < 2 V at high Id (gm collapses) or gain computed without gos (p.517 §8.6.5).
- [ ] Dual JFET offset (5-100 mV) not trimmed/servoed; loose Idss (e.g. 5-30 mA) not handled by a bias servo (p.513-515 §8.6.4).
- [ ] Source/degeneration resistors in a sub-nV JFET front end too large (110 ohm pair = 2 nV/sqrt(Hz)) (p.515 §8.6.4).
- [ ] Non-latching relays or plain contacts switching microvolt signals (coil current noise, heating drift, dry-circuit contact resistance) (p.515 §8.6.4).
- [ ] MOSFET discrete front end chosen for a < 1 MHz low-noise design (1/f knee 10-100 kHz) (p.520 §8.7.1).
- [ ] Differential-amp en taken at the pair's total current, or not multiplied by sqrt(2) (p.520 §8.8).
- [ ] Feedback-network resistors (R1 par R2) Johnson noise and in*R_par omitted in noninverting amp noise; 4kT/R2 current omitted in inverting amp (p.520-521 §8.8).
- [ ] CFB op-amp noise computed with equal in at both inputs (p.521 §8.8).
- [ ] Series bias-balance resistor at the noninverting input left unbypassed (adds Johnson noise) (p.521 footnote 70).
- [ ] TIA stability not checked against fT > f(RfCf)^2/f(RfCin); no cascode on a low-current high-gain stage (Miller) (p.499 §8.5.7).
- [ ] Photodiode bias resistor raised for noise until the diode saturates under ambient (night) light (p.499 §8.5.7).

### Ch.8 §8.9-§8.10: op-amp circuit noise, low-noise op-amp selection, signal transformers (pp. 521-537; lines 30490-33317)
- [ ] LT1028/LT1128 used above ~150 kHz? They have a broad 10-15 dB noise peak (150-600 kHz, max at 400 kHz). (p.524, p.527)
- [ ] LMV358/LMV321 on a noise-critical line without a fixed manufacturer: BJT and CMOS versions share the part number (en 39 vs 33 nV/rtHz, fc 8 Hz vs 7 kHz). (p.522-523 note Z)
- [ ] Current noise derived from Ib for a bias-cancelled op-amp: actual in is 10x-40x higher. (p.527)
- [ ] Bias-cancelled rail-to-rail-input part operated near a rail: Ib rises dramatically; tables do not show it. (p.527)
- [ ] FET-input Ib taken from the 25 C, mid-supply typical value: max can be 60x-800x typical and rises steeply with temperature and Vcm. (p.530)
- [ ] Very-low-Ib parts often carry an overly conservative max Ib from fast automatic testing; only high-priced parts get slow low-current tests. (p.530 fn 78)
- [ ] Some manufacturers misstate in (Table 8.3 italics are measured values); LT1012 datasheet in is in error; LM741/LM358 in unspecified; LMC6482 datasheet in cannot be correct. (p.527 fn 77, p.526 Fig. 8.58)
- [ ] LT1793/LT1792: datasheet 0.1-10 Hz p-p specs are 10x higher than the book's tabulated values - confirm which one you design with. (p.523 note u)
- [ ] Table 8.3 Ib units differ: nA for BJT (8.3a), pA for FET (8.3b). (p.527 fn 76)
- [ ] TLC4501A performs a 0.3 s self-calibration at power-on - output not valid until done. (p.523 note VV)
- [ ] LTC6078: Vos degrades near V+. (p.523 note RR)
- [ ] AD743 is NRND - do not design in without a supply plan. (p.532)
- [ ] CFB op-amp: inverting-input bias/noise current up to 10x the tabulated (lower) value. (p.527-529)
- [ ] Op-amp chosen on 1 kHz en alone while the band extends below ~10*fc. (p.530)
- [ ] Chopper/auto-zero op-amp used where the band extends well above 1-10 Hz: higher en and in than a low-noise BJT part. (p.531-532)
- [ ] Feedback/source impedance not below Rn/5 when the op-amp was chosen for low en. (p.529)
- [ ] High-dc-resistance sensor: Ib*Rs offset not budgeted. (p.529)
- [ ] Only one supply rail's PSRR checked: the other rail may be 25 dB worse (LT1012 +V) or < 10 dB at 1 kHz (LT6003 -V). (p.533)
- [ ] Self-heating ignored: OPA627 in SOIC-8 runs 34 C above ambient at +/-15 V; in-box ambient exceeds exterior. (p.530)
- [ ] Offset trimmed by unbalancing load resistors in a discrete differential stage: CMRR and current-source-noise rejection lost. (p.534)
- [ ] Hybrid op-amp run at closed-loop gain below its front-end gain without compensation: unstable. (p.535)
- [ ] BJT differential front end exposed to > +/-5 V differential without back-to-back diodes. (p.535)
- [ ] pnp input pair with unmatched Early voltages (ZTX951 VA = 120 V): CMRR degraded. (p.535 fn 85)
- [ ] Cascode front end driven out of compliance: it becomes a low-RON switch. (p.534 fn 83)
- [ ] Series resistor added to a low source impedance to "improve" NF (noise-figure fallacy). (p.536)
- [ ] Transformer coupling at audio frequencies without checking saturation, pickup, winding-resistance noise, bandwidth. (p.536)
- [ ] Feedback transformer carrying uncancelled dc bias current: core saturation. (p.537)
- [ ] Two LF breakpoints (coupling C and transformer Lmag) close together: motorboating. (p.537)
- [ ] Low-capacitance FET-input op-amps handled without ESD precautions. (p.532)

### Ch.8 §8.11-§8.16 + Review: transimpedance amplifiers, noise measurement, supply noise, interference/shielding/grounding (pp. 537-593; lines 33318-35540)
- [ ] TIA with large Rf and input capacitance but no Cf across Rf -> oscillation (p.537 §8.11.1).
- [ ] en treated as white at low frequency; datasheet 1/f region can be 10x-50x higher (p.538 §8.11.2).
- [ ] in taken from the 25C spec only; FET leakage (and in) rises steeply with temperature (AD743: 7 -> 40 -> 400 fA/rtHz at 25/80/125C) (p.538).
- [ ] JFET front end run at high VDS -> impact-ionization gate current and noise (p.538).
- [ ] Voltage noise assumed harmless in a current amplifier: en*omega*Cin noise current (p.539 §8.11.3).
- [ ] Cf sized for worst-case Cin but no output lowpass: out-of-band noise dominates at lower Cin (p.542 §8.11.7).
- [ ] Rf chosen so small that Iin*Rf < ~100 mV: amplifier noise exceeds signal shot noise (p.541 §8.11.6).
- [ ] Fast low-voltage op-amp chosen for TIA whose output (Iin*Rf incl. dc) exceeds its swing (p.543 §8.11.8).
- [ ] Parasitic capacitance of a single high-value Rf (~0.15 pF) sets an unintended fc; split into series string or cancel with pole-zero network (p.545 fn.99, Fig. 8.80).
- [ ] Shielded cable capacitance (~30 pF/ft) omitted from TIA Cin (p.547 §8.11.9).
- [ ] Bootstrap follower JFET noisier than the op-amp: its en acts on the full detector capacitance (p.547).
- [ ] Unbiased cascode at low signal current: re (25 kohm at 1 uA) with Cin rolls off at a few kHz (p.549).
- [ ] Cascode BJT chosen by beta alone; fT at uA currents too low (2N5089 ~2 MHz at 15 uA) (p.551).
- [ ] Biased cascode collector not trimmed to ground -> large dc offset at the high-gain TIA output (p.550).
- [ ] Guard/bootstrap driver too weak to drive a coax shield; add an emitter follower at a few mA (p.552 fn.112).
- [ ] Summing-junction node of a 1e9 ohm TIA routed on bare PCB (leakage); use Teflon standoffs (p.554 fn.119).
- [ ] Calibration current injected through a single high-value resistor: ~0.1 pF parasitic gives nA spikes on every edge (p.555).
- [ ] 1 V square wave into a TIA via 1 Gohm without compensation used for step-response trimming (p.555).
- [ ] Noise bandwidth taken as f3dB instead of ENBW (1.57x for one RC) (p.561).
- [ ] 1/f noise integrated from f1 = 0 (diverges) or budgeted with only the white density (p.563).
- [ ] Measurement filter with a single-pole upper rolloff: excess high-frequency noise included (p.564).
- [ ] Datasheet 0.1-10 Hz noise (p-p, asymmetric filter) compared directly with an rms budget without /6 conversion (p.564).
- [ ] LP2950/LM2931-class LDO with ceramic output capacitor and no series resistance -> oscillation (p.561 fn.124).
- [ ] LP2950 in a 9 V battery product without reverse-polarity protection (battery replaced backwards) (p.561).
- [ ] PRBS generator with XOR feedback can lock in the all-zeros state; use XNOR (p.560).
- [ ] Noise and signal measured together with an average-responding meter; use true rms (p.558).
- [ ] CMOS analog switch used to alternate noise-calibration sources; use a coax relay (p.558).
- [ ] Datasheet in used for an auto-zero or bias-cancelled op-amp without checking whether it was calculated rather than measured (up to 50x optimistic) (p.569).
- [ ] Current-noise measurement with Rs too small (Johnson noise or en dominates) or too large (bias-current saturation, oscillation, 16 Hz bandwidth at 10 Gohm/1 pF) (p.570).
- [ ] Femtoamp jig not guarded; humidity/fingerprints create leakage (20x from breath) (p.573 fn.135).
- [ ] Plastic pushbutton or unwiped glass reed switch as integrator reset (static/triboelectric charge injection) (p.573).
- [ ] Averaging (sine-calibrated) meter used on noise without the 1.13 (+1 dB) correction, or used on sine + noise (p.569).
- [ ] Simple lowpass used to rescue a near-dc signal from 1/f noise instead of modulation + synchronous detection (p.575).
- [ ] Square-wave (switching) phase detector responding to odd harmonics where a sine-reference multiplier is needed (p.575 fn.141).
- [ ] Lab bench supply assumed quiet because of its spec (0.2 mV rms is 46 dB above a low-noise preamp floor); switchers and wall-warts orders of magnitude worse (p.578).
- [ ] Brute-force 10,000 uF across supply terminals instead of a capacitance multiplier (p.579).
- [ ] Capacitance multiplier without enough Q1 headroom for full-load ripple, or without the base-circuit bead/resistor (oscillation) (p.579).
- [ ] Metal enclosure relied on to stop low-frequency magnetic pickup (p.581).
- [ ] Mu-metal single shield saturating in a strong field (no low-mu outer layer) (p.582).
- [ ] All-ceramic bypass network resonating with rail inductance at tens-hundreds of MHz (no lossy electrolytic) (p.582).
- [ ] Low-level amplifier supply daisy-chained after a power stage, or load current returned through the input ground (p.582 Fig. 8.124B).
- [ ] Small single-ended signals between instruments on different outlets (>= 100 mV ground difference) (p.583).
- [ ] Shield of a floating mV transducer grounded at both ends (p.585 Fig. 8.131).
- [ ] Isolation amplifier output not lowpass-filtered (carrier/clock residue) (p.587).
- [ ] Long guarded cable instead of a follower near the high-impedance source (p.587).
- [ ] Precision analog trace next to fast logic with > 0.02 pF coupling at 0.5 V/ns and 0.1 mV resolution (p.588).

### Ch.9 §9.1-§9.5: linear regulators, heat and SOA, ac line to unregulated dc (pp. 594-636; lines 35530-38215)
- [ ] Discrete regulator without current limiting: a short draws beta x I_opamp = 1-10 A (p.597 §9.1.1C).
- [ ] Pass transistor outside the feedback loop (divider "corrected" for Vbe) gives temperature- and load-dependent output (p.597 §9.1.1B).
- [ ] Crowbar without a fuse: if the fault also disables current limiting, the SCR sinks very large current (p.598).
- [ ] Open voltage-setting resistor makes the output soar (Exercise 9.2 context) - crowbar covers it (p.598).
- [ ] 723: forgot output trim although Vref spread is 6.8-7.5 V (p.599).
- [ ] 723 input dips below 9.5 V (including ripple) - reference drops out (p.599).
- [ ] 79xx negative regulator given only 78xx-sized bypass caps - may oscillate (p.601 footnote 12).
- [ ] Split supplies without output reverse-protection Schottky diodes - latchup/reverse polarity (p.602).
- [ ] LM317 divider too high in value - minimum-load (10 mA) not met and adj-current (up to 100 uA, 5 uA tempco) errors (p.603).
- [ ] LM1117 / LDO used with too little output capacitance (<10 uF) (p.603).
- [ ] LM317 top resistor copied from datasheet examples (240 ohm): the 10 mA minimum load is not met at zero external load (p.604-605 §9.3.4A, fn 16).
- [ ] Rated regulator current assumed available at high V_in - V_out (can fall to 20% near V_in(max)) (p.603).
- [ ] Large output/bypass capacitance with no output->input protection diode on a regulator whose input can be crowbarred or shorted (p.606 §9.3.4F; p.619 §9.3.12I).
- [ ] ADJ-pin noise capacitor added without its protection diode (required above 10 uF) (p.619).
- [ ] Soft-start circuit assumed to ramp from 0 V (it jumps to 1.25 V or ~1.8 V first) (p.607).
- [ ] High-voltage dc-dc converter powered abruptly - startup overshoot/arcing (p.606 fn 18).
- [ ] Negative regulator tab grounded: the 79xx/337 tab is the input (p.613 §9.3.12A).
- [ ] Ceramic output capacitor on an ESR-compensated LDO (LP2950/2951, LM2940) - oscillation, zero or raised output (p.617).
- [ ] CMOS LDO exposed to > 5.5 V (or to unsuppressed off-board transients) (p.616).
- [ ] Battery life computed from light-load Iq while the pnp LDO sits in dropout (ground current x10) (p.616).
- [ ] Automotive input without load-dump-rated regulator (13.8 V rail spikes >= 50 V) (p.618).
- [ ] Regulator noise specs compared across different bandwidths or rms vs p-p (p.615, 619).
- [ ] Reverse-polarity: split-rail fault or asymmetric power-down reverses a rail; no reverse Schottky per output (p.616).
- [ ] Heatsink sized from a 25 C case-temperature power rating (unrealistic) instead of from R_thJC and real ambient (p.624 fn 33).
- [ ] Heatsink mounted fins-down/horizontal without derating (+20-70% R_thSA) (p.627 fn 35).
- [ ] BJT pass transistor used near its voltage limit without second-breakdown SOA check (p.627).
- [ ] Uninsulated transistor tab (collector/drain) touching a grounded chassis/heatsink (p.624, 626).
- [ ] Line cord without protective earth bonded to chassis (p.629).
- [ ] No fuse, or fuse sized from average current, or fast-blow fuse on a capacitor-input supply (p.630-631).
- [ ] Fuse holder wired with hot on the accessible (front) terminal (p.631).
- [ ] Non-safety-rated capacitors across the line or line-to-ground; X capacitor without bleeder (<1 s) (p.631-632).
- [ ] MOV without thermal cutout (p.632).
- [ ] Exposed line-voltage terminals inside the enclosure (use heat-shrink) (p.632).
- [ ] Transformerless offline supply in an instrument that interconnects with other equipment (p.632).
- [ ] Unregulated input trough below regulator minimum at low line/brownout -> 120 Hz dips on output (p.632).
- [ ] Filter capacitors oversized (transformer heating, rectifier stress) or tolerance (-30%/+100%) ignored (p.633-634).
- [ ] Transformer rms rating equal to the dc load current (needs ~2x) (p.633).
- [ ] No RC snubber across the transformer secondary (leakage L + diode recovery spikes, 120 Hz interference) (p.634).
- [ ] No bleeder/minimum-load resistor on an unregulated supply (output soars; stays charged after power-off) (p.634).
- [ ] Switcher noise judged only at its output pins; the load sees ground-current spikes (p.636).
- [ ] Mains-filter performance judged from 50 ohm/50 ohm attenuation curves (p.631 fn 41).

## 7. Process / lifecycle guidance
### Ch.5 §5.10-§5.14: choosing precision op-amps, auto-zero amplifiers, DMM front ends, difference amplifiers (pp. 319-356; lines 18822-21122)
- Component screening and supply continuity (p.347 §5.12.5E): stage = component qualification -> activity = screen critical parts to a tighter-than-datasheet limit (supplier or in-house) -> deliverable = screening spec and incoming-inspection record -> exit criterion = screened parts meet the product limit (e.g. 50 pA input current vs 500 pA datasheet worst case); risk item: no guarantee of process continuity; mitigation: lifetime buy (AOE-2097).
- Incoming inspection for a parameter-dependent design (p.344 fn 59): verify JFET VGS relationship (Q1 at 0.7 mA below Q2 at 1.4 mA) on each batch (AOE-2090).
- Incoming inspection to settle datasheet plot vs table conflicts (AD8273 CMRR, p.355 fn 68) (AOE-2116).

### Ch.5 §5.15-§5.17 + Review: instrumentation and fully differential amplifiers (pp. 356-390; lines 21123-23445)
| stage | activity | deliverable | exit criterion | source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| design | tally all precision error contributions in an error budget; decide strict worst-case vs pragmatic treatment per term | error budget table | dominant error sources identified; unspecified parameters shown insignificant or covered by test | p.388 Review ¶B-¶C |
| component qualification | set up incoming testing for critical unspecified/typical-only parameters | incoming test procedure | parts meet the assumed values | p.388 Review ¶C |
| prototype / DVT | abuse-test input protection on the bench (overvoltage, fast HV steps) before shipping | abuse-test report | no damage, no latent degradation | p.363 §5.15.5 |
| production test | validate the overall circuit, subassembly or instrument at final test when the error budget is overfull | final-test limits | measured performance within spec | p.388 Review ¶C |
| in-service | periodic in-system offset/gain calibration via relays, constants stored in software | cal procedure + stored constants | gain error < 0.1%, offset <= 10 uV | p.360 §5.15.4 |

## 8. Coverage log

Rule ids were assigned per piece and renumbered contiguously at assembly. Final id ranges per piece:

- Ch.7 Oscillators and Timers + Review (pp. 425-472; lines 25111-27190): AOE-2324 to AOE-2421 (98 rules)
- Ch.8 §8.1-§8.8: noise fundamentals, BJT/JFET low-noise design (pp. 473-521; lines 27191-30489): AOE-2422 to AOE-2536 (115 rules)
- Ch.8 §8.9-§8.10: op-amp circuit noise, low-noise op-amp selection, signal transformers (pp. 521-537; lines 30490-33317): AOE-2537 to AOE-2604 (68 rules)
